I used to be the one setting things up—choir rides, garden swaps, snacks for the kids’ basketball stuff. Now I’ll just sit there staring at my phone for hours and… I don’t even answer people. I just can’t make myself care enough to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It takes me forever just to bathe and get dressed, get to the door. My family says I move like I’m underwater.\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still… I pace in the hallway and wring my hands. I don’t know. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory problems led you to make mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I started messing up the road-closure paperwork, or just not finishing it. And with traffic safety stuff, I’d second-guess myself so much I didn’t feel like I could trust what I was doing.\n\nI stopped being reliable, I guess. People noticed. My family did too… I’d pulled back from choir and volunteering and them, and they finally kind of… brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to solve?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I worry some, I guess. But I’m not sure it’s like that. I haven’t really been keeping track of it.\n\nDoctor: Did these mind-blanking and memory problems start around the same time as the sadness and loss of interest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… around the same stretch of time, I think. For months now, when I’ve been waking up sad and guilty and not really eating… that’s also when the voices and the messages from trucks or radios have been happening.\n\nI don’t like talking about it, but… yes, it all kind of overlaps.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voices or messages, do they tell you that you’re guilty or deserve to be punished?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it feels like that. Like they’re pointing at everything I didn’t do right.\n\nI keep going over things with my friend, and choir, and my family… all the ways I failed them. And then it feels like I’m just… useless. Like maybe I deserve whatever’s coming.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices or messages ever happen when you are not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Before it got really bad, I was still going to work and church and everything, but I’d already started hearing my name sometimes.\n\nAnd… I thought signs, or the radio, were kind of… sending things about me. That went on for a few weeks, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were the voices or messages happening for at least 2 weeks during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I think… yes. It was a few weeks, before things got so low. I was still making it to work and church, but I was already hearing my name and feeling like signs or the radio were sending messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech has been hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. People have told me I’m not making much sense, but I don’t always notice it when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Even when you are not feeling depressed, do you still find yourself emotionally flat or not caring about being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to be the one setting things up—choir rides, garden swaps, snacks for the kids’ basketball and all that. Now I can just sit there for hours looking at my phone and… not even feel like answering people.\n\nDoctor: Were there times when you heard voices or believed the radio or signs were sending messages about you, but you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there were a few weeks like that. I wasn’t really down yet, not the way it got later. I was still going to work and church, but I was already hearing my name sometimes, and… thinking the radio or signs were meant for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
